( 9 ) के उपबंध, तमिलनाडु मरपेन्टाइल बैंक लि टूटीकोरिन पर 
18 जून , 1992 मे 17 सितम्बर , 1992 मक नगा बैक के नियमित

का . पा . 2013 -~-यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है 
कि लोकहित में यह आवश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में जो . जी . एस .- V 
से जी . जी . एस . - VII तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये पाइप लाइन 
तेल तथा प्राकृतिक गैस आयोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 
____ और अतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनों को बिछाने के 
प्रयोजन के लिए एतदाबद्ध अनुसूची में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का 
अधिकार अजित करना आवश्यक हैं । 

अतः अब पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन ( भूमि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का अर्जन ) अधिनियम , 1962 ( 196 : का 50 ) की धारा 
3 की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
ने उसमें उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का अपना प्राशय एनद्वारा 
घोषित किया है । 

शर्ते कि उक्त भूमि में हितबद्ध कोई व्यक्ति ; उस भूमि के नोचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए आक्षेप समक्ष प्राधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गेस आयोग , निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग, मकरपुरा रोड़, बड़ौदा- 9 को 
इस अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । 


Provided that any person interested in the said land may , 
within 21 days from the date of this notification , cbject to 
the lay ng of the pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority , Oil & Natural Gas Commission , Construction & 
Maintenance Division , Makarpura Road , Vadodara -390C09 . 

And every person making such an objection shall also state 
specifically whether he wishes to be hear in person or by 
legal Practitioner.

पास भागोग, निर्माण और दखभाल प्रभाग , मकरपुरा रोड, बड़ोना

___ का . मा . 2020 ---- यत : केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत 
होता है कि लोकहित में यह आवश्यक है कि 
गजरात राज्य में जी . जी . एम . 5 से जी . जी . एस . 6 तक 
पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये पाइपलाइन तेल तया प्राकृतिक गैस 
आयोग द्वारा विछाई जानी चाहिए । 

- और अतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनों को बिछाने के 
प्रयोजन के लिए एतदपाबद्ध अनुसूची में वणित भूमि में उपयोग का 
अधिकार अजित करना आवश्यक हैं । 

अतः अब पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन ( भूमि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का अर्जन ) अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) को धारा 
3 की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
ने उसमें उपयोग का अधिकार अर्जित करने का अपना प्रायर एनद्वारा 
घोषित किया है । 

बशर्ते कि उक्त भूमि में हितबद्ध कोई व्यक्ति , उस भूमि के नीचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए प्राक्षेप सक्षम प्राधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैस प्रायोग , निर्माण और देखमाल प्रभाग , मकरपुरा रोड, बडौदा - , 
को इस अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । . 

____ और ऐसा आक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिर्दिष्टतः यह भी कयत 
करेगा की क्या यह बह चाहता है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यक्तिगत रूप से हा . 
या किसी विधी व्यवसायो की मार्फत । 


And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying such 
pipeline, it is necessary to acquire that right of user in the 
land described in the schedule annexed hereto ; 


Now , therefore , in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub -section (1 ) of section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) Act, 1962 
( 50 of 1962 ), the Central Government hereby declares its 
intention to acquire the right of user therein ; 


Provided that any person interested in the said land may , 
within 21 days from the date of this notification , object to 
the lay ng of the pipeling under the land to the Competent 
Anthority , Oil Natural Gas Commission, Construction & 
Maintenance Division , Makarpura Road , Vadodála- 390009. 


And every person inaking such an cbjection shall also state 
soccifically, whether he wishes to be heard in person or by 
legal practitioner .

का . पा . ::026 . - -यत: केन्द्रीय सरकार का यह प्रतीत 
होला है कि लोकहित में यह मावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में पाई। 
एडा पे पावरा ई पी एस तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिए पाइपलाइन 
तेल सपा प्राकृतिक स भायोग वा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 
___ भीर यतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनो को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एतप्पायन मनु मूबी में बणित भूमि मे उपयोग पा प्राधिकार 
पभित करना प्रावश्यक है । 

प्रतः प्रब पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइप लाइन ( भूमि में उपयोग में 
प्रधिकार का भर्जन ) अधिनियम , 1962 (1962 का 50 ) को धारा । 
को उपधाग द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय साफा ने 
उसमे उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का प्रपना आशय एतदद्वारा 
घोषित किया है । 

बशर्ते कि उस माम में हितमंद कोई व्यक्ति , उस भूमि में नीचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए, माक्षेप सक्षम प्राधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैस पायोग, निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग, मकरपुरा रोड , मडावा - को 
इस अधिसूचना को तारीख से 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । 

बार ऐसा भाषोप करने वाला हर :क्ति विनिर्दिष्ट . यह भ म मा 
करेगा कि क्या यह यह कहना है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यकिगत मा में छ . 
या किसी विधि व्यवसाय 4 मार्फत ।

की उपधारा द्वारा प्रवत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार मे

पोषित किया है । 


माते कि उक्त भूमि में हितब कोई व्यक्ति, उस भूमि के नीचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए भाक्षेप सक्षम प्राधिकारी सेल सपा प्राकृतिक 
गैस भायोग , निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग , मकरपुरा रोड , बगदा- 9 को 
इस मधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा ।

करेगा कि क्या वह यह पाहता है कि उसकी मुनवाई व्यक्तिगत रूप से हो 
या किसी विधि व्यवसायी की मार्फत ।

पा आ 03 : -- पत केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि 
लोकहित में यह प्रावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य मे इळयू रम्य टी पी 
से डाइर नवी तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहम के लिये पाइपलाईन तेल तथा 
प्राकृतिक गैम प्रायोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और रन , यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनो को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए तद पावन प्रमुसूची में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार अजित 
करमा प्रावश्यक है । 

प्रत , अध, पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाईम ( भूमि मे उपयोग के 
माधिकार का प्रर्जन ) अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धार्ग की उप 
घाग द्वारा प्रदत शक्तिया का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार ने उममे उपयोग 
का अधिकार मंजिन करने का अपना प्रशय एतद्द्वारा घोषित किया है । 

बशत कि उक्म भूमि में हितबद्ध कोई व्यक्ति , उम भूमि के नीचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए माक्षेप समक्ष प्राधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक गैस 
मायोग , निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग, मकरपुरा रोड, बडौदा को हम 
अधिसूचना की सारीख मे 21 दिनों के भीतर कर सकेगा । 

और ऐसा प्राक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिर्दिष्ट न: यह भी कथन 
करेगा कि क्या वह यह पाहता है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यक्तिगत रूप से 
हो या किमी विधि व्यवसायी की मार्फत ।

नयु तक पेट्रोलियम में परिवहन के लिये पाइपलाइन तेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैस प्रायोग द्वारा बिछाई आनी चाहिए ।

के लिए सपाबद्ध अनुसूची में वर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार 
अजित करना प्रावश्यक है । 

अतः प्रम पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन ( भूमि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का प्रर्णन ) अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3

उममें उपयोग का अधिकार अर्जित करने का अपना प्राशय तदद्वारा 
घोषित किया है । 

अभर्ते कि उक्त भूमि में हिनबद कोई व्यक्ति, उम भमि के नीचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए प्राक्षेप मक्षम प्राधिकारी तेल तथा प्राकृतिक

का . प्रा 2037 - ~-यत. केन्द्रीय सरकार का यह प्रतीत होता है कि 
लोकहित में यह आवश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य मे नाग-1 से जी एन ? 
मय तक पंदोलियम में परिवहन के लिये पाइपलाइन मेल तथा प्राकृतिक गंम 
पायोग द्वारा मिठाई जानी चाहिए । 

और अत . यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनों को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एतपापड अनुसूची में वर्णित भूमि मे उपयोग का अधिकार 
अजित करना पावण्यफ है । 

प्रत प्रब , पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन ( भूमि में उपगंग के 
अधिकार का अर्जन ) अधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 3 की 
उपधारा गरा प्रवत्त शक्तियो का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय गरकार ने 
उममें उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करन का अपना माशय एतद्वारा 
घोषित किया है । 

बगतं कि उक्त भूमि में हितब कोई व्यक्ति , उस भूमि के नीचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए प्राक्षेप गक्षम प्राधिकारी सेल नथा प्राकृतिक 
गम भायोग , निर्माण और देखभाल प्रभाग, मकरपुरा रोड, बड़ीदा- 9 का इम 
पधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिनो के भीतर कर सकेगा । 

और गेमा प्राक्षेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति यिनिर्दिष्टतः यह भी कथन 
करेगा कि क्या यह वह पाहमा है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यक्तिगत रुप में 
हो मा किमी विधि व्यवमायी की मार्फत ।

फा , प्रा . 20 : - पतः केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि लोग 
हित में यह प्रावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में नाडा -- 1 से जी एन ए क्यू 
तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिए पाइपलाइन तेल नया प्राकृतिक गैम 
प्रायोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और प्रतः यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐसी लाइनों को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एतद्पाबत मम सूची , में वणित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार 
मर्जित करमा प्रावश्यक है । 

मतः मम पेट्रोलियम और खनिज पाइपलाइन ( भूमि में उपयोग के 
अधिकार का प्रजेन ) मधिनियम , 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) की धारा 
3 की उपधारा द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्रीय सरकार 
ने उसमें उपयोग का अधिकार अजित करने का प्रपना प्राशय एतवार। 
घोषित किया है । 

बार्ते कि उक्त भूमि में हितब कोई व्यक्ति ; उम भूमि के नीचे 
पाइपलाइन बिछाने के लिए पाक्षेप सक्षम प्राधिकारी टेल तथा प्राकृतिक 
गैस पायोग , निर्माग और देखमान प्रभाग , मकरपुरा , बडौदा - ५ 
को इस अधिसूचना को नागेख से 21 दिनो के भीतर कर सकेगा । 
____ और ऐगा माझेप करने वाला हर व्यक्ति विनिविष्टनः यह भो कथन 
करेगा कि क्या यह वह चाहता है कि उसकी सुनवाई व्यक्तिगत रुप मे 
हो मा किसी बिधि व्यवसायी की मार्फत ।

New Delhi, the 14th July, 1992 
S . O . 2041. . - Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of the petroleum from GGS -I to Jn. Point in 
Gujarat State pipeline should be laid by the Oil & Natural 
Guw Commission . 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline , it is necessary to acquired that right of user 
in the land described in the schedule annexed hereto . 


का . प्रा . 2041. - -यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि लोक 
हित में यह प्रावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में जी . जी . एस . - 1 से 
मम्मन बिन्दु तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिये पाइपलाइन तेल मया 
प्राकृतिक गैम प्रायोग द्वारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 

और माता पा प्रतीत होता है कि प्रेमी लाइनो को बिछाने के प्रयोजम 
के लिए एतदपाबद्ध मनुसूची में कणित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार प्रणित 
करना प्रावश्यक है । 


Now , therefore , in exercise of the powers cooferred by 
sub - section ( 1 ) of the section 3 of the Patroleum and Mine 
rals pipelines ( Acquisition of Right of User in the land ) Act, 
1962 ( 50 of 1962 ), the Clentral Government bereby declares 
it s intention to acquire the right of user therein

New Delhi, the 14th July , 1992 
S. O . 2042. --- Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that it is necessary in the public interest that for tho 
transport of the petroleum from Kalol Fiold to Collector line 
in Gujarat State pipelinc should be laid by the oil and 
Natural Gas Commission . 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline , it is necessary to acquire that right of user 
in the land described in the schedulo annexed hereto . 

Now , thcrefore, in exercise of the powors conferred by 
sub -section ( 1) of section 3 of the petroleum and Mine 
rals Pipelines ( Acquisition of Right of User in the Land ) 
Act , 1962 ( 50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby dec 
laros it s intention to acquire the right of usor thorein 

Provided that any person Interested in the said land may, 
within 21 days from the date of this notification , object to 
the laying of tho pipeline under the land to the Competent 
Authority , Oil & Natural Gas Commission , Construction and 
Maintenance Division, Makarpura Road, Vadodara - 390009 . 

And every person making such an objection shall also 
state specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or 
by legal Practitioner .

का . मा , 5042: - यतः केन्द्रीय सरकार को यह प्रतीत होता है कि लोक 
हिस में यह मावश्यक है कि गुजरात राज्य में कलोल क्षेत्र से मालमटर लाइन 
तक पेट्रोलियम के परिवहन के लिए पाइपलाइन तेल तथा प्राकृतिक गैस 
मा योग धारा बिछाई जानी चाहिए । 


और मन यह प्रतीत होता है कि ऐमी लाइमों को बिछाने के प्रयोजन 
के लिए एतदपावर अनुसूची में पर्णित भूमि में उपयोग का अधिकार अजित 
करमा प्रावश्यक है ।

नई दिल्ली , 14 जुलाई, 1992 
का . आ . 2043 . - - जबकि केन्द्र सरकार यह अनुभव करती है कि सार्वजनिक हित में यह प्रावश्यक है कि पैट्रोलियम पदार्थ 
एवं प्राकृतिक गैस लाने के लिए मान्ध प्रदेश में पाइप लाइन परियोजना के अंतर्गत पासलपुरि- 9 से पासलपुरि- 8 तक तेल और 
प्राकृतिक गैस पायोग द्वारा बिछाया जाना है । 

और यह भी अनुभव करती है कि इस कार्य के लिए एमके माथ संलग्न विवरणी में निर्धारित भूमि पर प्रयोक्ता का अधिकार 
ग्रहण करना मावश्यक है । 

प्रतः पैट्रोलियम एवं खनिज पाइप लाइन भूमि पर प्रयोक्ता का अधिकार ग्रहण अधिनियम 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) 
केख 3 के उपखण्ड ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्र सरकार एतदद्वारा उम पर प्रयोक्ता का अधिकार 
ग्रहण करने की आशा की घोषणा करती है । 

बशर्ते कि उक्त भमि में अपनी रुचि रखने वाला कोई भी व्यक्ति अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिन के भीतर भूमिगत 
पाइप लाइन बिछाने के विरोध में अपनी प्रापसि सक्षम प्राधिकारी तेल और प्राकृतिक गैस पायोग , के , जि , प्रोजक्ट , 
भूसेकरणा , कार्यालय , राजमंदि , प्रांध्रप्रदेश में दर्ज करा सकता है । 

और ऐसी भापसि दर्ज कराते समय किसी भी व्यक्ति को यह विशेष रूप से निर्दिष्ट करना होगा कि वह ग्यक्तिगत 
रूप से प्रथषा विधिव्यवसायक के माध्यम से अपना मत प्रस्तुत करना चाहता है ।

का . आ . 2044: - -- जयकि केन्द्र मरकार यह अनुभव करती है कि सार्वजनिक हित में यह आवश्यक है कि पैट्रो 
लियम पदार्थ एवं प्राकृतिक गैस लाने के लिए पान्ध्र प्रदेश में पाइप लाइन परियोजना के अंतर्मत पोरी 2 में इपीएस पोरी तक 
तेल और प्राकृतिक गैस आयोग द्वारा बिछाया जाना है । 

और यह भी अनुभव करती है कि उस कार्य के लिए इसके साथ संलग्न विवरणी में निर्धारित भूमि पर प्रयोक्ता का 
अधिकार ग्रहण करना आवश्यक है । 

अतः पेट्रोलियम एवं खनिज पाइप लाइन भूमि पर प्रयोक्ता का अधिकार ग्रहण अधिनियम, 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) 
के खण्ड 3 के उपखण्ड ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्यिों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्र सरकार एतदद्वारा उस पर प्रयोक्ता का अधिकार 
ग्रहण करने की मंशा की घोषणा करती है । 

अशत कि उक्त भूमि में अपनी रुचि रखने वाला कोई भी व्यक्ति अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिन के भीतर भूमि

भूसंकरणा , कार्यालय , राजमंद्रि , मान्ध्र प्रदेश में दर्ज करा सकता है । 


और ऐसी आपत्ति दर्ज कराते समय किसी भी व्यक्ति को यह विशेष रूप से निर्दिष्ट करना होगा कि वह व्यक्तिगत रूप

का . आ . 2045 . - - जबकि केन्द्र सरकार यह अनुभव करती है कि सार्वजनिक हित में यह आवश्यक है कि पैट्रोलियम पदार्थ एवं 
प्राकृतिक गैस लाने के लिए आन्ध्र प्रदेश में पाइपलाइन परियोजना के अंतर्गत चिन्तलपल्ली 1 से 14 " टी पाइन्ट तक तल 
और प्राकृतिक गैम आयोग आग बिछाया जाना है । 

और यह भी अनुभव करती है कि उस कार्य के लिए इसके साथ मंलग्न विवरणी में निर्धारित भूमि पर प्रयोक्ता का 
अधिकार ग्रहण करना आवश्यक है । 

प्रत . पेट्रोलियम एवं खमिज पाइप लाइन भूमि पर प्रयोक्ता का अधिकार ग्रहण अधिनियम 1962 ( 1962 का 50 ) के 
खण्ड 3 के उपर पड ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए केन्द्र सरकार एतदद्वारा उस पर प्रयोक्त का अधिकार 
ग्रहण करने की अंशा की घोषणा करती है । 

बशर्ते कि उक्त भूमि में अपनी रुचि रखने वाला कोई भी व्यक्ति अधिसूचना की तारीख से 21 दिन के भीतर भूमि 
गत पाइप लाइन बिछाने के विरोध में अपनी आपसि सक्षम प्राधिकार तेल और प्राकृतिक गैम आयोग प्रोजेक्ट , भूसंस्करण , 
कार्यालय , राजमंद्रि, मान्ध्रप्रदेश में दर्ज करा सकता है । 

और ऐसी आपत्ति दर्ज कराते समय किसी भी व्यक्ति को यह विशेष रूप से निर्दिष्ट करना होगा कि वह व्यक्तिगत 
रूप से अथवा विधिव्यवसाय के माध्यम से अपना मत प्रस्तुत करना चाहता है ।

AWARD 
This is a reference made by the Central Government un 
10 ( 1 ) ( d ) of the Industrial Disputes Act , 1947 ( 14 of 1947 ) 
for adjudication of the dispute as to whether the action of 
the management of MS. S . Lal and Co , ( ubsequently re 
named as M /s. Essel Mining and Industries Ltd . ) , owner 
of Julling Longalota Iron Mins denying employment to Smt. 
Mali Dei, Water Carrier w . e. f. 12 - 10 - 79 is justified . 

2 . It is needless to make it reference to the pleading of 
the parties as becase the same have been succintly stated by 
this Tribunal in the earlier order dated 7 - 3 - 87. 

On filing of the written statement by the partics, heering 
on mrit was taker up . Whilc challenging the maintainability 
of the reference the management plcacked that Smt, Mali Dei 
was never employed as a workmun under it . On an appraisal 
of the evidence adduced by the parties, this Tribunal while 
deciding the reference maintainable held that the woikinan 
was an employee of the management and that she was refus 
od of employment. However, the reference could not be 
fully and finally disposed of and award passed as evidence 
was lacking as to whether the workman hud been painfully 
omployed subsequent to her disengagement. Feeling aggrieved 
by this order the management moved the Hon ble High 
Court in a writ which was dismissed in 1991. As the Hon ble 
High Court directed to dispose of the case withjn three 
months from the date of appcarancc of the parties , they 
wore given opportunity to lead cvidence on the question 
of gainful employment, As the inanagement did not 
turn - up the workman gave evidence whereafter argument was 
hcard and date was fixed for passing Award . Subsequently 
the managoment appeared and prayed to allow it to

AWARD 
This is a reference made by the Central Government u / s 
10 ( 1 ) ( c ) of the Industrial Disputos Act, 1947 ( 14 of 1947 ) 
for adjudication of the dispute as to whether the action of 
the management of Jiling Longalota Iron Mines of M / S . 
S . Lal and Co . Ltd . (subsequently renamed as M / s. Essel 
Mining and Jpdustries Ltd . ) denying employment to Sri 
Bharat Mabanto , Water Carrier with effect from 7 - 9 - 79 is 
justified , 

2 . It is needless to muke & reference to the pleadings of 
the parties as because the same have been succially stated 
by this Tribunal in the earlier order dated 9 - 3 -87. 

On filing of the written statement by the parties, hearing 
on mer it was taken -up . While challenging the maintainability 
of the re- orence the management pleaded that Sri Bharat 
Mabunto way never employed as a workman under it . On 
an appraisal of the evidence adduced by the parties this 
Tribunal while deciding the referenco maintainable held 
that Sri Mahanto was an employee of the management 
and that his services were terminated . However, the 
veforence could not be fully and finally disposed of and 
Award passed as evidence was lacking as to whether thic 
wothman hitd been gainfully employed subsequent to his 
disengagement, Fooling aggrived by this order the manage 
ment moved the Hon ble High Court in a wriy which 
was dismissed in 1991. As the Hon ble Court directed 
to dispose of the case within threonionths irum the 
date of appearance of the parti : 9, Jicy were given 
opportunity to Icad evidence on the question of gainful 
cinpleincnt. As the management did nui turn -iip the work 
man have evidence whereafter argument was heard and date 
was fixed for passing award. Subsequently the manager 
men Eppeared and prayed to allow it to participato in the 
hcaring tefoie passing award. Saving heard the porties 
order was passed allowing the management to have its say 
before final adjudication of tho dispute. Thereafter the 
management cross - examined the workman examined earlier 
and also examined one witness on its behalf . 

3 . It is contended on behalf of the managment that in 
vlow of the observation of tho Hon blo Supreme Court this 
Tribunal ought to have allowed it to lead evidence both on 
the question of maintainability of the reference as well as 
on merit. That having not been done all the issues involved 
in the reforence can not be answered in either way . I am 
not prepared to accept such contention of the management 
for the reasons set -out below . 


Nos . 2 and 3 . In view of such evidence , I am inclined to 
hold that the action of the management in doing away with 
the job of the workman has no sanction under law and 
henco the same has to be held to be illegal and unjusti 
fied . 

5 . The next question that falls for consideration is 
whether after being denied of servico tho workman was 
employed elsewhere or remained unemployed . 

The workman in his evidence has stated that being 
denied of employment since 7 - 9 -79 he is unemployed . On 
tho other hand , it is the case of the managment that the 
workman has been working under a contractor of Kalinga 
Mining Corporation as a Mazdoor . To prove this the 
management has examined MW -2 who during cross -exami 
nation would admit that he only heard from the villagers 
about the emplcyment of the workman in the aforesaid 
Mines. MW - 3 denied to have any knowledge regardithg 

cmploymght of the workman elsewhere. This being tho 
sum total of evidenco led by the parties , I am inclined 
to hold that since disengagement by the management the 
workman has remained unemployed . 

6 . On a conspectus of the evidence as discussed above , 
I hold that denial of employment to the workman being 
illegal and unjustified , he is entitled to reinstatement with 
full back wages. He bo reinstated and back wages be paid 
to him from the date of his removal from service i. . ., 
7 - 9 - 79 . Payment be made within three months from the 
date of publication of publication of the Award , 

The carlier order passed by this Tribunal on 9 -3 -87 bo 
treated as part of this Award . 
Dictated and corrected by me.

ORDER 
1. This is a oference under Section 10( 1)(d ) of the Indust 
ral Disputes Act, 1947 made by the Central Government 
for adjudication of the dispute vide Order No. L - 26011 / 13 / 
80 - D JI ( B ) dated 13th October , 1980. The schedule of 
reference is as follows - 
" Whether denial of employment to Shri Bharat Mohanto , 

Water Carrier by the Management of Jilling Long 
lota Iron Mine of M / S , Lal and Co . Ltd . from 
7 - 9 - 79 is justified ? If not, to what relief is he 
entitled ? " 


The finding dated 9 - 3 -87 of this Tribunal on two issues 
As aforesaid was challenged by the management in the Hon 
blo High Court by way of writ. Having heard the parties . 
their Lordships refused to interfere with the findings and 
dismissed the writ . Againat the said order of the Hon ble 
Court , the management filed a special leave petition before 
the Apex Couit . From the Xerox copy of the order of 
the Hon ble Supreme Court, it transpires that the manage 
ment withdrew the said special leave potition . While allow 
ing the prayer for withdrawal tho Hon ble Supreme Court 
observed that it is open to the parties to agitate whatever 
lagues there are in the reference before the appropriate Court . 
Tho management intending to take advantago of this obser 
vation submitted that by necessary implication the Hon ble 
Supreme Court has set- aside the judgment passed by the 
Hon ble High Court. This submission, as I have already 
said , can not be accepted . If at all the management had 
valid legal points to challonge the findings of the Hon ble 
Court, the snecial leave petition would have been allowed . 
From the aforesaid observation of the Hon ble Supreme 
Court what I gather is that it is for the parties to agitato 
the remaining issue which is yet to be decided by this 
Tribunal 

4 . From the avallable evidenco it is now to be decided 
whether denial of employment to the workfilman is legal 
and justificd , Tho management on whom onus lies to 
prove the same did not choose to lead any evidence in that 
regard . On the other hand . the workman in his evidence 
has stated that as he demanded for enbancement of wages , 
his services were terminated by the management. This 
ovidence of his has been corroborated by workman witness

denied employment. Per atter was referred to the Manage 
ment and when no action was taken he raised this before 
the Assistant Labour Commissioner for his action and inter 
vention . The Labour Enforcement Officer ( C ) enquired into 
tho matter on 2 - 12 - 79 and submitted his report (copy en 
closed as Annexure - I). 

3 . The Management s stand is that the order of reference 
made at the instance of the North Orissa Workers Union 
16 not maintainable . This Union is a foreign Union and 
is not at representative Union of the Company s workers. 
The workman in question was at no point of time in the 
employment of the Management and the question of refusal 
of his employment by the Management at any time does not 
Arise . In the early part of the year 1979 when the North 
Orissa Workers Union had raised the dispute with the 
Management, the Management vide its letter dated 19th 
October, 1979 had categorically stated and denicd that the 
workers named by the said Union had at no point of time 
been in their employment. The other allegations mache in 
the written statement by the workman have been denied . 

4 . The first question for consideration is whether the 
second party was a workman under the Management. In 
this connection we have four witnesses for the workman 
and one for the Management. The Second Party has stated 
in his evidence that he was the Water Carrier under the 
First Party for about four years continuously when his 
services were terminated as he chemanded enhanced wages . 
Ho reported the fact to the Labour Officer who enquired 
into the matter , After enquiry he was given employment 
for one week only . In his crossX -examination it has been 
elicited that his duty hours were from 5 A . M . to 12 Noon 
and from 3 P .M . to 5 P . M . The witness No. 2 bas similarly 
stated that the second party was working as a Water Carrier 
under the Management. This witnoss was also a workman 
under the Management. He has of course been dismissed 
from service . But that by itself would not detract from 
the value of his evidence . The witness No. 3 was also a 
workman under the Company. He has stated that the Second 
party was a Water Carrier under the First Party for about 
four years . This witness has also been chargeshceted on 
allegation of non - performance of his duty . That fact alono 
also would not make his evidence unreliable . The Labour 
Enforcement Officcr as witness No. 4 it appears bad held 
an enquiry into the complaint made by the North Orissa 
Workers Union and during that enquiry he had recorded 
the statements of this workman and another witness vide 
Ext. 1 . This Officer no doubt looked into some of the 
records of the Company but did not find the name of the 
worker in those register. The witness for the Management 
has stated that the second party never worked as a Water 
Carrier under the Management. This Manager has also 
statod that the Labour Officer had inspected some records. 
The best evidence for the Management would have to produce 
the records containing the name of this worker and to show 
that this workman was nover in their employment. In fact 
this witness in cross - oxamination has admitted that B 
form register contained all the names of the employees. 
That register if produced would havo indicated the correct 
position . For non - production of that register adverse in 
ference has to be drawn against the Management. On a 
consideration of the evidence adduced by both the parties 
I am inclined to hold that the second party was a workman 
under the First Party . This being the finding the evidenco 
of the workman that his services have been terminated has 
to be accepted . 

5 . The next question is about the maintainability of the 
reference. The case of the Management is that the North 
Orissa Workers Union is a foreign Union and is not the 
representative of the workman . It is also said to be a 
Union not recognised by the Company. On behalf of the 
workman no evidence has been ackduced to show that tho 
workman was a member of the North Orissa Workers Union , 
It can not therefore be said that the Union could havo espoused 
his cause . But the fact remains that this is a dispute as 
mentioned in tho reference between the employer and the 
workman . As provided in Section 2 ( a ) of the Industrial 
Disputes Act the workman in case of denial of employment 
could raise their dispute individually and as such the dispute 
can not be said to be not an industrial dispute. The reference 
is thereforo maintainable . 

6 . Whon considering the question of lelief it is seen that 
the parties have neither pleaded por adduced gay evidence 19

compulsorily ictiriog lim from scrvice is fal in laxy and 
against the principles of natural justice . Paragraph 34 of 
ONGC Conduct and Discipline and Appeal Regulation , 1976 
prescribes minor as well 99 najor penalties. Compulsory 
retirement from schuice is a major penalty and so guch 21 
punishment can be imposed upon A11 employee . It was sub 
mitted that Paragraph 11 of the Regulation is not complied 
with in this case because no sholy causc notice was served 
upon the concerned workinan before he was visited with 
the penalty of compulsoily retiring hini from service. There 
is no merit in this submission because para 41 of the regula 
tion does not require tho gervice of said show cause notico . 
Paragraph 41( a ) of the regulations gays thut notwithstanding 
anything contained in Regulit ons 36 to 411 where a penalty 
is imposed on the ground of conduct which had led to his 
conviction of a Criminal change , the disciplinary authority 
may consider the circumstances of each case and pass guch 
an order as it deems fit. So this provision does not require 
the service of show cause notice before imposing the penalty 
upon the employees on the ground of conduct leading to his 
conviction on a criminal charge. In the instant case the 
concemned workman was charge -sheetect . He had pleaded 
guilty to the charge levelled against him . The learned 
Magistrate had held him guilty and so he was proved to 
havo committed those offonces and consequently he was con 
victed . Now what is challenged is the order of compulsory 
retiring the concerned workman from service . That order 
is at mark 181 and 22 1. This order will go to show that 
the authority had taken into consideration his conduct lead 
ing to his conviction on a criminal charge . This is evidence 
from paragraph 1 of the order. It also appears from this 
order that a show causc notice was served upon the concern 
ed workman and he had replied to the same. His reply atd . 
17 . 12 .84 was duly considered by the authority because there 
is a positivo mention to this effect in the order itself. Thus 
the authority concerned had taken into consideration the 
facts mentioned in the cr minal charge levelled against the 
concerned workman and the written representation dtd . 
17 . 12 . 84 filed by him before passing the final order. The 
concerned workman has under tho list at Ex. 6 produced 
some documents. Some documents arc cxhibited . Ex. 16 is 
tho xerox copy which bears tlie s gnatuic of the concerned 
workman . It is dtd . 17 . 12 . 84 . this was, therefore the iepresen 
tation made by the concerned workman in iesponse to show 
cause notice served upon him . This representative makes a 
mention uhout the letter No. AMD VD -1- 23 0 - 180 ) 463 ) 
66 dtd , 12 . 12 . 84 . This show cause notice is at Ex. 10 . This 
will therefore go to show that the show cause Ex. 10 was 
< uly received by the concerned workman and that bc had 
submitted his reply hereto . Thus the contention of the se 
cond party that the first party has committed breach of 
principles of natural justice or the regulations by not serving 
finy show cause notice upon the corcerned workman is not 
coitoct. It was submitted thut Ex. 16 was written by the 
P . A . of Shri Bhagwan Saheb and below that his signature 
was taken . Thus he has admitted his signature below Ex. 16 . 
It is easy for any workman to say that the representation 
was not dicated to him or that he had not read it. Thus it 
is clearly proved that the first party had served a show cause 
notice upon the concerned workinan before tak ng fipal 
decision of compulsory retiring him from service, As already 
said by mo Regulation 11 does not require the service of 
such a show cause notice. Even thon in the instant case the 
show cause was served upon the concerned vaskman . He 
had replied to the same and it was taken nto consideration 
us is ovident from the order of punishment, 


gay that any order passed under this Act shall not be 
regaided as its disqualification suffered by the offender. This 
Section was interpreted in this reported case . It was held 
that this provision must be placed out of way and that it 
is not possible to accept the reasoning that the employee 
could not be dismissed from service for reason of tbo pro 
vision contained in Sect on 12 of the Probation of Offen 
ders Act. In that case their Lordships had reproduced some 
Observations made by the lcuined Magistrate for the puspose 
of holding that the penalty of dismissal from service imposed 
upoil the employee was . . . . . . . . . . . . . It was submitted that 
learned Magistrate has made some observations which should 
be taken into consideration . The learned Magistrate has 
observed that it was the yirst offence committed by the con 
cerned workman , This is not a ground for justification for 
reduction in tho punishment imposed upon the concerned 
workman . He had also iaken in to consideration the report 
of the Probation Officer had observed that the concerned 
workman had committed offence on the ground of financial 
constraints and that he had no criminal mind while committ 
ing the offenco. In the reported case the family circumstanc 
es and calamities with which the person was vinted woro 
detajled by the learned Magistrate . These facts aro absent 
in the instant casc and in the order of the learned Magistrate . 
Tlacreafter my attention was drawn to thọ caso of H . P . 
Thakur vls. State of Gujarat and others reported in 20 GLR 
at page 109, wherein his Lordship of the Gujarat High Court 
had held that before imposing the economic death penalty 
of dismissal or removal icom service the disciplinary autho 
rity should apply his mind to vital considerations which are 
enumerated in the judgement . It was not a case whercin an 
employoe was convicted by a criminal offence . It was not a 
Cahy wherein the offender was dealt with any provision of Pro 
bution of Offcndere Act and so this decision is not helpfull for 
deciding the justification or otherwise of the order of 
compulsory retiring the concerned workman from servico. 
The learned advocate of the first party has drawn my atten 
tion to the case reported in 1983 ( II ) LLJ at page No. 85 . 
In that case the workman was dismissed after his misconduct 
of faith was proved . He had put in 17 yrs. of unblemighed 
service . The question for consideration was whether the 
arrangement of dismissal from sev.ce was disproportionate 
having regard to the 17 vrs. of unhlemnished service. In the 
judgement rendered by the Madras High Court it was held 
that plea of reinstatement was untenable and punishment im 
posed was on concessional side. In that case theft was of 
canteen coupons of the value of Rs. 24 . 48 , whereas in the 
instant case the concerned workman had committed cr‘minal 
offenco in respect of Rs. 712 . 39 . In the reported case tho 
punishment was modified ag one of termination of service 
with the direction to the dianagement of pay one month s 
salary to the workmen . So the punishment of dismissal war 
moulified one of tçimination of service and this way re 
garded as on concessionul side in the reported case . There 
after my attention was drawn to the case reported in 1990 
CLR p . 541. It is a Supreme Court case . In that case it 
was held that a criminal trial of conviction is one thing and 
sentence is another and departmental punishment for miy 
conduct is yet a third one . In this reported case instead of 
convicting the offender the Court way pleased to release 
him on probation of good conduct . It was therefore , held 
that such an order does not affect the conviction which ic 
mains untouched , and the stigma of conviction is not obli 
terated. This observation very aptly applies to the facts of 
the present case . It was again held that in the departmental 
proceeding the delinquent could be dismissed or removed 
or recluced in runk on the ground of conduct which had lead 
to bis Conviction on a criminal charge . So all these punish 
ment could have been imposed in the instant case . The 
concerned workman was not dismissect from service . His 
servicey were also not terminated , but he was compulsorily 
retired from service. It was also hel. 1 that benefit oi pioba 
tion of offenders Act does not mean that even y convict 
cannot be dism ssed from service . Their Lordships have refer 
red to the case reported in 1987 . . SC case 39 wherein 
the punishment of dismissal was converted into that of re 
moval from service so that it may held the convict to secure 
future employment in another establishment . So relying on 
that decision their lordships altered the punishinent of dis 
misso ) from service into removal from service . It is im 
portant to note that in this reported cusc thc cmployee was 
a Constable in the CRPP. He had not committed any 
offence . It appears that he and some other Constables had 
forced entry into the room of other constable . It appears 


8 . The second party has drawn my attention to some 
decisions. My attention was ist drawn to the case of 
Shanker Dags vs. Union of India and Another reported in 
1985 2 SC cases 358. It is a judgement rendered by the 
Supreme Court. In that case a Governinent servant was in . 
volved and \o his case WAS considered under Article 311 (2 ) 
2nd proviso ( a ) of the Constitution of India , whereas thiy 
article is not applicable in the instant casc . In the instant 
case the concerned workman was given the hencfit of pro 
vision of Probation of Offenders Act. So instead of sentenc 
ing him he was reloased on conrlition to maintain peace and 
law and order for a period of two vears . He was also di 
rected to execute a bond of Rs. 2000 /- for that 
period . He was also informed that if any default is commited 
So far as this cond tion is concerned , he will he required 
to appear before the Court to receive the punishment in 
respect of the offences for which he was held guilty . It was 
submitted that S . 12 of the probition of offenders, Act

that he had himself observed that som persons werd ben 
tenced hy the Courſ. However , the Session Judge while at 
holding the conviction released him under the Probation of 
Offonders Act. This is the latest decision of the Supreme 
Court. It had not directed reinstatemeni of the person . There 
fore , there is no justification for bulding that the punish 
ment of compulsory retirement from service imposed upon 
the concerned workman is on higher side or is not propor 
tionate with the gravity of the misconduct proved to have 
been committed by h m , in view of his admission of guilt 
before a competent Criminal Court. It must be said that tho 
penalty imposed by the first party or on concessional side 
because the concerned workman was neither dismissed nor 
terminated from scrvico . Henco the second party has failed 
to prove that the order compulsorily rotiring the concerned 
workman from service is not justified . Therefore , the present 
reference deserves to be dismissed . So I pass the following 
order.

AWARD 
The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour in 
exercise of powers conferred upon them by clause (d ) of 
sub - section ( 1) and sub- section (2A ) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 ( 14 of 1947) and by their 
Order No. L -22012(57) /89-IR (C . II ), dated 7th September, 
1989 have referred the following dispute for adjudication 
by this Tribunal : 
"Whether the action of the management of Deulbera 

Colliery of M /s . S . E . C . Ltd ., At / P . O . Deulvera , 
District : Dhenkanal in retiring Shri Dwari Behera , 
Mining Sirdar from service w . e. f. 16 - 7 - 1987 is 
justified ? If not, to what relief the workman 

concerned is entitled ? " 
2 . Shortly stated the case of the workman is that he 
entered into service in Deulbera Colliery of the South 
Eastern Coalfield Ltd . on 1 - 1 - 1960 . Though his actual date 
of birth as mentioned in the school leaving certificate is 
2 - 1- 1929 but in the Official records of the management 
wrong entry has been made showing his date of birth to be 
16 - 7 - 1927 which is quite wrong and is based on nõ material. 
During course of his employment he appeared two examina 
tions, namely , Mining Sirdar certificate examination and Gas 
Testing Certificate examination held on 1970 and 1972 res 
pectively . Necessary forms were forwarded by the manage 
ment before such examinations were held . Necessary entries 
of those forms including the date of birth were properly filled 
in and forwarded by the authority . Accordingly , in the 
certificates issued to the workman his date of birth has been 
mentioned as 2 - 1 - 1929. But relying on the wrong entry of 
the date of birth in the official record the Management 
passed orders that the workman would retire from service 
on attaining the age of superannuation on 16 - 7- 1987 . So , 
the workman agitated his grievance whereafter the manage 
ment agreed that it would constitute an Age Determination 
Committee to ascertain his actual date of birth . Accordingly , 
a Committee was set-up which held its sitting on 27 -5 - 1988 
and in obsence of any material accepted the date of birth of 
the workman as mentioned in the relevant register of the 
management. Fmboldened with the finding of the Com 
mittee the management retired the workman from his service 
though infact he had some more months to serve. With 
the help of the Orissa Coalfield Labour Union he raised a 
dispute which was admitted to conciliation and the same 
having failed a leference was made to this Tribunal fur 
adjudication . It is , therefore , urned that by the time ol 
llegal retirement the workman had 18 months to serve as 
because his date of birth is 2 - 1- 1929 but not 16 - 7 - 1927. 

3 . Refuting the claims of the workman the management 
has pleaded inter alia that the actual date of birth of the 
workman is 16 - 7 - 1927 which finds mention in the Official 
register . If actually he was born on 2 - 1 - 1929 and he was 
in possession of the school leaving certificate he should have 
produced the same when his medical examination was held 
in 1962 i.e ., after his entry into service . In the medical 
examination repor as well as in the service sheet the work 
man had put his signature wherein his date of birth finds 
mention and thereby he has acknowledged all the entries 
made therein . In this view of the matter it is pleaded that 
the workman is etiopned to say otherwise that his date of 
birth is not 16 - 7 - 1927 but 2 - 1- 1929 . Challenging the cor

6 Now coming to the evidence of the workman , I find 
that his date of birth as mentioned in his school leaving 
certificate , Ext. 1 is 2 - 1 - 1929 . The authenticity of this cer 
tificate has not been challenged by the management in couito 
of hearing The other two certificates , Exte. 2 and 3 arc 
Sirdar s Certificate and Gas Testing Certificate where it is 
mentioned that the workman was born on 2- 1 - 1929 . In 
clause ( B )(ix ) of Ext. 4 it is provided that Mining Sirdar 
ship , Winding Engine or similar other statutory certificates 
whicro the Manager has to certify the date of birth will be 
teated as authentic . In both the certificates , Exts , 2 and 3 
referred to above , the concerned authority has certified that 
the present workman was born on 2 - 1- 1929 . On an evalua 
tion of the cvidence of the parties , I am of the opinion that 
the case of the workman stands in a better footing. The 
two certificates , Exls . 2 and 3 has been issued when the 
ditpute regarding date of birth of the workman was not even 
in its mother s womb .

7 . In view of my discussions made above , I hold that date 
of birth of the workman is 2 - 1 -1929 but not 16 - 7 - 1927 . So , 
he would have retired on attaining the age of superannuation 
in the month of January 1989 but the management relying 
on the wrong entry of the date of birth in the service regio 
ter retired him on 16 - 7 - 1987. In the circumstance , there 
fore , the workman would be deemed to have continued in 
service till 31 - 1 - 1989. Fiom 16 - 7 - 1987 to 31- 1 - 1989 he shall 
be paid all his wages and the said period be taken into con 
Sideration for calculating his retirement benefits , if any . The 
rayment of wages for the aforesaid period be made within 
three months from he date of publication of this Awaid .

2 . The case of tho uniou in brief is that the 46 concerned

till nationalisation and thereafter under the Eastern Coal

The inanagement just to deprive the workers of their legiti

through contractors . But the management had no contract

ully licensed contractor . That in view of the principles laid 
down by the Hon ble Supeme Court these cmployces were 
the direct employees of the management. The present work 
mca were allotted work relating to mining operation and 
these works come under the prohibited category . The work 
men through their union approached the management to 
employ them , but the management turned deaf cars,

mately the Ministry of Labour has referred the dispute to 
this Tiibunal for adjudication .

nanagement has denied all the material averments of the

ruary, 1975 and then the services were torminated .

they should be treated as regular employces on the 
principley laid down in the cases reported in 1990 
L- ah. 1. C . page 1968, 1988 LLJ(I ) Page 730 and 
1978 I LJ(II) page 397.

AWARD 
The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour in 
exercise of the powers conferred on them by clause ( d ) of 
sub -section ( 1 ) and sub -section (2A ) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, has referred the following dis 
pute to this Tribunal for adjudication vide Ministry s Order 
NO. L - 24012 ( 154 ) / 87- D . IV ( B ) dated the 29th April, 1988 . 

SCHEDULE 


the domestic enquiry proceedings and other relevant docu . 
ments on the plea that all the documents and record of 
proceedings are missing. But in the judiciai proceeding on 
such submission the finding of the domestic enquiry cannot 
be upheld and it cannot be held that the domestic enquiry 
was properly and fairly held . As the management has fail 
ed to produce the record of the domestic enquiry proceed 
ings and other relevant documents . I find that the domestic 
enquiry was not properly and fairly held . I further find that 
the charge levelied against the workman were not proved by 
any cogent evidence, So the findings of the alleged domestic 
enquiry is hereby set aside. 

9 . 1 the result I find that the reversion order of 
Sri Manager Singh w . e . f. 6 - 10 82 was rot justified . He 
shall be given all the pecuniary benefits and other service 
benefits holding that he was not reverted to the post of 
Grade-III Clerk w .e.f. 6 - 10 82 . 
This is my award . 

N . K . SAHA, Presiding Officer

" Whether the action of the Management of Nutandanga 

Colliery of M / s. E . C . Lid ., P . O . Nutandanga . Dis 
trict Burdwan (WB ) in denying regularisation to 
S / Sri Balai Bouri, Mason Mistry , Jaldev Pan , Rabi 
1al Ghosh , Mahabir Gope, Haradhan Dewasi, Dija 
Das, Rodi Kal, Adhir Dome, Tarapada Mal, Kalia 
pada Das and Nabani Dhan Pal, ali Mazdoors , is 
justified ? If not, to what relief are the concerned 

workmen entitled 34 
2 . The case of the union in brief is that all the 11 work 
men of this case were continuously working at Nutandanga 
Colliery under Eastern Coalfields Ltd ., in underground jobs 
as deployed by the local administration through contractors 
both before and after take over of the non -coking coal mines 
and continued to be so deployed till 1973 . Such deployment 
of workers on jobs undergrounds connected with the coal 
production was violative of the provisions of the Contract 
Labour (Regulation & Abolition ) Act. There was serious hue 
and cry from trade unions and as well as political part es in 
Parliament. The Government decided to abolish the coniragi 
labour system and the contractors workmen were taken on 
the roll of the company sometime in 1974. In Nutandanga 
Colliery there were some other workers working under con 
tractors and they were taken on the roll of the company. 
But for the reasons best known to the company the present 
11 concerned workmen were left out. 

The attempts of conciliation ended in failure . The matter 
was sent to the Ministry of Labour, Government of India , 
Ultimately the dispute has been referred to this Tribunal for 
adjudication . 

3 . There was no reason for such discrimination done by 
the management in case of the present 11 workmen . So the 
union has prayed that the present workmen may be absorbed 
in the roll of the management w . e. f. 1st April, 1974 and 
they may be paid the arrcar wages and consequential bene 
fits.

4 . The management has filed written statement contending 
inter - alia that the present Reference is not maintainable . The 
workmen never worked under the managenent. There was 
no employer and employee relationship . So the question of 
regularisation of the present workmen does not arise . The 
present workmen were the employees of different contractors 
and were engaged for various jobs as per work order and 
terms of contract . 

5 . It is the clear case of the Union that the present work 
men were the contract labours under contractors at Nutan 
danga Colliery from before nationalisation of non -coking 
coal mires and they continued to be so deployed even after 
nationalisation . This fact has not been challenged from the 
side of the managernent. On the other hand in para 7 of the 
written statement of the management it has been clearly 
admitted by the manajement that the present workmen wero 
the employees of difffiept contractors engaged for various 
jobs as per work order and terms of contract . So considering 
the inaterials on record and the facts and circumstance , I 
have no heritation to find that the prerent workmen were 
never the direct emrloyees of the manasernent both before 
and after nationalisation . But I find that the present workmen 
worked as contract labours for various jobs underground 
continuously from before nationalisation and they continued 
to brcn deplavad even after nationalization ,

9 . 1 After having heard at length both the parties , the 
following issues come up ior decision . 
( 1 ) Whether the charges levelled aga nst the workman 

are vague, baseless o - the same are really serious 
and grave ? 
Whether the domestic enquiry has been properly

have been observed during the course of enguiry ?

are proved beyond doubt ?

whether there was fair play in awarding punish

12 . Issue No. 2 -- Reasoning - Dec sion : 

12 1 Issue No. 2 : It is known that there has been no 
statutory provisions relating to procedure to hold the domestic 
enquiry by the employers in dealing with cases of misconduct 
alleged against their employees though need not conform w 
all the judicial proceed ngs, they must however satisfy essen 
tials of natural justice. l he Supreme Court laid down guiding 
principle through judicial pronouncements , the saire is that 
enquiry should be conducted with scrupulous regard from 
requirement of rules of natur justice that is without bisa 
and by giving the delinquent employee an opportunity for 
adequately representing nis case and that the enquiry must 
be held honestly and bonafide with a view to determine 
wheher the charge against employee concerned is proved cr 
not, the enquiry does no 1, come an empty formality . The 
pr nciples of natural justice over periods, is now crystalised 
into four principles of justice (1962 II LLJ . Cal .) name y : 
" (i) Opportunity for both the contesting parties to be 

heard ; 
( ii ) Hearing before impartial tribunal so that no man 

can be a judge of his own cause ; 
( ii ) Decision made in good faith ; and 
(iv ) An orderly course of procedure . 

Apart from this , no other prirciple of natural just.cz 
is known in modern jurisprudence ." 

( 1962 II LLJ. Cal.) . 
12 .2 The Supreme Court observed in case of Sur Enamel 
and Stamping Works Ltd . ( 1963 II LLI) as under : 
" An enquiry cannot be said to have been properly held 

unless 
(i) tht employee proceeded aga nst has been in or 

med clearly of the charges levelled aga nst h m , 
(ii ) Witnesses are examined ordinarily in the presence 

of the employee - 11 respect of charge, 
( ü ) The employee is given a fair opportunity to 

examine wi nesses, 
( iv ) He is given a fair opportunity to cross examine 

witnesses. 
( v ) He is given a fair opportunity to examine witnesses 

including himself in his efence if he so desi. es 

on any relevant matter, and 
( vi) The Enquiry Officer records his findings with ro 

asons for the saine in his report.” 


(5 ) Whether any interference by Arbitrator is called for ? 
10 . Issues -- Decision - Resoning : 

10 . 1 Issue No . 1. It is sevealed from the proceedings of 
domestic enqujiy that this workman abused his super .or 
Executive Engineer , and behaved in disorderly manner and 
that particulars of abus ve language was specifically given in 
the charge and I find it is not vague charge . The workinan 
nowhere denied on this count, nor he made contention that 
the charge was vague. 


10 .2 On charge of rintons and disorderly behaviour of the 
workman , I observe that there has been mention in charge 
sheet this specific charge . The behaviour on this court was 
not caused by any provocat on by the officer concerned i.e . 
Executive Engineer . This was not case of the workman that 
there was any provocation to him . It is nowhere stated that 
the concerned Executive Engineer kad indulged in any be 
haviour which might have caused provocation in workman s 
subord nate behaviousr , aisorderly hehaviour or riotuos be 
haviour (of the workman ) , The evidence on record shows 
that this workman threatened the superior officer and he , 
therefore , cannot escape the consequences of the plea 
that it was done in fit of anger - See Dhragandhra Chemical 
Works 1963 LLJ 2 . This was argued by workman at one 
stage . 


10 . 3 There has been available specific evidence, as is 
found enquiry proceedings before me, connecting th s work 
man the act of disorderly behaviour of holding of hand by

itself in presence of cther err . ployecs and the workman 
abused the officer , the specific language fised by workman was

man . 


12 .3 When I apply the above test to find out the status

at W - I and W - II does not come to h s rescue to give him 
prop . The workman did not advance any argument on this 
court. I find the enquiry was proper and it has been n ac 
cordance with the principles of natural justice and it is also 
in accordance with other judicial pronouncement,

shouted at superior officer i.c . Executive Engineer in piesence 
of othe . employees when tne. r was no provocation from ihe 
officer concerned . This is sufficient to say that the work 
man s behaviour was „ndecent and it was not orderly behavi 
our . The denial of workman on this count do not help work 
man and absclve him of ine charge . The charge No. 3 
against the workman was proved to the extent that the work . 
man threatened, abused superior. This has been confirmed by 
the witnesses duly cross examined by the workman , thal 
abuses were hurled ( by workman ) at Executive Engincer. It 
has becn very undesirable of the workman to bui] abuses, 
without any provocation , though even provocation does not 
absoive hun of this charge . This was unbecoming of any 
workmin - employee of any organisai on . It cannot be said 
that the workman was innocent. 

13 .2 Since the Enquiry Officer has not relicd on information 
given by Shri Vijay it canno : be said tha : principles of 
natural just ce were not followed . The version of the work

13. 3 The issue No. 3 is answered that charges levelled 
against workman are proved beyond doubt . 


14 . 5 One it is establ shed that the charges are proved , the 
question arises whether the punishment awardeci is just. fied 
or not. I find no employee worth the name has a place in 
any organisation if he is found guilty of gross misbehav our. 
No officers can take work from their subordinate f gross 
misbehaviour is not condoned or no action is taken on this 
sort of behaviour. As already mentioned in previous paras, 
there was no provocation to workman who abused in pre 
sence of other employees n filthy language the officer -Super 
Visor- Executive Eng neer . Tht abuses have been spelled out 
by the wiinesses. The affected officer lodged complaint in 
writing of such insulting behaviour or the part of this work 
man . This complainant was cross examined by ths work . 
man . It cannot be imagined that oficer of status of Execu 
tive Engineer will even give a false report or complaint 
which is involving hs own insult. I find thc dismissal (of 
workman ) by General Manager, W . C . Ltd , Nagpur Area , 
Nagpur is jus .ified it being fair one . The dismissal oider 
datcd 17 - 5 - 1990 issued by General Manager , W . C . Ltd .,

ansver the reference accordingly . Once I find the disinissal 
of workman jus ified , there is no question of grant of any 
relief on this count. 

14 .6 It has been pleased that the award of dismissal is dis 
proportionate and or t is shockingly harsh . The workunan 
harped on provisions of Section 11- A of ID . Act, 1947 which 
gives power to Labour Courts , Tribunals and National 
Tribunals to give relief to the dismissed workmen . Tile title 
of the Sect on 11- A of I. D . Act indicates that Arbitralor is 
excluded from operation of Section 11- A . This being position , 
I am not able to act in terms of Section 11 - A , My powers aie 
limited to the reference before me. In view of this legal 
position I am unable !o . ve any relief to the workman con 
cerned . 

14 .7 I have already answered the reference on affirmative. 
Hence my award .

officets , I do not fnd any ustification on the part of

7 . Tho managemco hag a contention that tho service 
conditions of officers and workmcn employees are entirely 
different and the i orhnun employces are not entitled 10 
claim the benefits on par with the Bank officers . But the 
fact remains that in other Nationalised Bank the work 
men employecs are being paid additional annouat for doing 
work relating to closing of accounts on par wile the oficers . 
Of course it is not teruca as closing allowance but as over 
time wages. But the addition1 payinent is made for doing 
tbc work relating to closing of accounts half ycurly and 
annually . So this contention of management is also devoid 
of merit . 

8 . From the discussions made above it is clear that the 
naragement Bank is discriminating the workinen employees 
in the matter of payment of closing allowance for doing 
the work relating to closing of accounts. On a consideration 
of all the aspects stated above I have no hesitation to hold 
that the action of management in denying tho payment of 
closing allowanco to workmen employees 18 highly discrimi. 
natory and negation of natural justice . The workmen en 
ployees are therefore entitled to get Rs. 140 cach for each 
closing work on par with the amount paid to samc category 
of employees in IOB as per Ext. W2 circular , 

9 . In the result, an avard is passed holding that the action 
of the management of Syndicate Bank in not payirg 
closing allowance to Award staff attending to tlic closing 
work on par with the officers of the Bank is unjustificd and 
the said workmen are accordingly entitled io get closing 
allowance @ Rs. 145 per employeo for each closing of 
accounts .

2 . The case of the sponsoring union , Bihar Colliery 
Kamgar Union , as disclosed in the writton statement submitted 
on behalf of the concerned workmen , details apart, is as 
follows : 

Gobardhan Mahato and 296 other concerned workmen 
wboso Bames and address have been disclosed in the annexur 
to the schedule of reference, have been working as slurry 
removal workmen contiguously in Sudamdih Coal Washery 
sinco long with unblemishod record of service . They have 
beco working in permanent and prohibited category , of job 
of removal of slurry in Sudamdih Goal Washery continuously 
and in the process have put in attendance for 240 days in 
oach calendar year. They have been working on the job of 
removal of slurry within the precinct and premises of the 
washery under the supervision and control of the manage 
ment, and all implements for execution of the job are being 
supplied by the management. Although they aro working 
under the management, they are being paid much less wages 
than prescribed in N . C . W . As and that too through alleged 
intermodiaries. The alleged intermediaries have neither any 
power to engage any workman nor remove any workman 
from service nor have they any power to supervise and 
control over the job of the concerned workmen . The alleged 
intermediaries have also no powor to prescribe the requisito 
work load for the concerned workmen . As a matter of fact 
the requisite work load of the concerned workmen have 
been prescribed and settled by the management. Slurry fs 
- Athing but fine particles of good quality of washed " coal 


which flows out of the washery and soutles down in the 
ponds tanks excavated by the managenieat. Hon blo 
Cupreme Court has held that slurry 18 nothing but coal. The 
concerned workmen are working in the ponds tanks with

. 3 . The case of the management of Sudamdih Coal Washery

The present reference is bad in law and pot maintainable , 
as there exists as relationship of employer and employce bet 
ween the management and the persong referred to in the re 
ference . 


The prosent reference is also in violation of the provisions of 
thc Contract Labour (Regulation and Abolition ) Act and based 
on misrepresentations on fucts . The substantive case or wo 
management is that Sudamdih Coal Washcry is one of tho 
Cool Washerics of M / s. B . C . C . Ltd . which is a Government 
Company within the moaning of Sec . 617 of the Company s 
Act and it is wholly financed by the Central Government . 
The Cool Washery in question liko all other Coal Washerios 
have been constructed for the beneficiation of coal (improving 
the qualiiy of coal by removing ash , moisture etc .) and to 
make it suitable for use in the steel plants for steel melting, 
Tho process of washing /bencficiation of coal in washeries is 
such that some fine particles of coal flow out along with the 
special liquid solution used for the purpose of washing coal , 
This is culled slurry which is collected in the ponds situated 
outside the washery premises and the water which is a cons 
tituent of slurry is removed and the fine particles or coal are 
salvaged and stacked . When these fine particles of coal flow 
ing out of the washery with slurry assume below a given size / 
quality the slurry is called tailings or rejects . Since fine par 
ticles of coal and their quality above specified size represent 
washed coal which can be used in steel plants , the manage 
nont of washeries makes constant efforts / endeavour by instal 
lation of necessary cquipment and modernising the equipment 
and taking various other methods having a bearing on the re 
of coal and quality as a part of blurry and to eliminate tho 
levant technology to reduos flowing out of such line particles 
samo as far as possible . When the ash content and other in 
purities in the composition of slurry exceed a specified norm , 
the slurry assumes the form of tailings/ rejects and it is no 
longer necessary to collect it. Sudamdih Coal Washery was 
constructed in 1980 through a contractor on a turn -key con 
tract basis . At the time of construction of the Washory somo 
workers were employed by tho contractor. When tho construc 
tion of the washery wag completed, there was no longer any 
work for the contractor or his men . At that time, Shri A . K . 
Roy was the M . P . of Dbanbad having been elected in 1979

ing union , Bihar Colliery Kamgar Union and he is still the

before the Dy. Commissioner, Dhanbad , demanding provision

10 . Shri Ghosh has outlined the function of the washery

of energy . The principal function of the washery as underlined

management,

slutty ponds. The solid portion of coal settle s down in the

that the workmen of the ex -contractor were given with 
choice of forming a Co - operative Sociсty tuing which there 
would be no work for them . The manageulent his stated 
that Co -operative Society is a registered body and produced 
its rules ( Ext . M - 7 ). But these rules by themselves do not 
show that the concerned workmen are menibers of this 
Society . There is no positive evidence to indicate that the 
concerned workmen are the members of tliis Society . 

Then again , the management has asserted that contractual 
job of removal of slurry was awaided to the Co-operative 
Sociсty aforcaid and the Co -operative Society has been 
cxccuting the work by employing its own workoren and the 
concerned workmen are the workmen of this Society . 

M / , B . C . C . Ltd ., 19 a public sector underiaking. Admit 
tedly , tender is required to be floated for awarding con 
tractual work . But in the present case no evidence has been 
laid to show that tender was floate . The inunngement has 
produced some bills purported to have been submited by the 
Kanigar Shramik Sahayog Samity . Strange though it pay 
he seen these bills are printed on the statione , of M / 8. 
B .CC. Ltd . (Ext. M - 4 ). Some wage- shcets Exts . M -3 series 
ADD 11- 18) have been produced hy the management to show 
that Sahayog Samity has paid wagos to its wurkmen . The 
sponsoring union has asserted that all these arrangements are 
paper arrangement in order to cancuiage the real position 
and suppress the fact that the coccerned workinen are the 
worlmca of Sudamdih Coal Washery . I have already held 
that Wię place of work of the workmen working in the 
slurry ponds belongs to M / s. B . C . C . Ltd . and that the job 
of Yuriy removal is an integral part of the establishment 
of thy management of Sudandih Washery . Slurry removal 
wo from the slurry ponds is undertaken for the purpose 
of culbilshment of the management of Sudamıın Washery . 

15 . The sponsoring usion has clumed that the concerned 
workmon have heen working in the slurry pou as sluity 
removal workmen and that they have been doing their work 
under the direct control and supervision of the manajement 
and that the work implements are supplied by the manage 
ment. These statements of facts have been denied by the 
management in its written tatement. Shri Ghosh has also 
Jenied this But two of the concerned worknieni who exa 
mired themselves in this case have asserted ihat tlicy have 
hecy working under the direct control and supervision of 
the management and that the management fius been pro 
vidiog them with work implements. The Project Officer of 
Sudamdih Washery by lctter dated 2 -8 - 89 directed the 
Secretary of Sahayog Samity to submit the list of workmen 
working in lurry ponds alongwith recent photography (Ext. 
W - 1) . Similar request was made carlior by letter dated 
26 /28 - 7 - 89 (Ext W - 2 ) and letter dated 20 - 7 -89 (Ext, W - 3 ). 
The Project Oficer fixed the work - load for the workmen 


working in tho slurry pond . at 3 . 1 cubic motio por head 
per day by letter dated 11 / 12 - 7 - 89 (Ext. W - 4 ). The man 
agement directed the Samity to take action for excavating 
slurry pond properly (Exts . W - 5 and W -6 ). The management 
* advised the Samity to resort to dumping of sluiry first in 

the stack yard in railway siding (Ext. W - 7 ). The manage 
ment also directed the Samity not to inci case manpower 
without its coascot (Exts . W - 8 and W - 9 ). All thcsc evidence 
indicate that the concerned workers working in the slurry 
pond are broadly under the control of the establishment of 
the management. It has been held by Hon ble Supremo 
Court that when a worker or group of woj . crs labours to 
fender service which forms integral part of service of 
1,101her and utilised as such , then the other is, in fact, the 
employer oven though the employment is brought about by 
or through some other person acting under the supervision 
And control of the former ( 1978 Lab . I. C . 1264 between 
Hussainbhai Vs. The Alath Factory Tezhilali Union and 
others ). This position has been relied on by Calcutta High 
Court in the case reported in 1991 Lal), I . C . 1062 (Divi 
sional Railway Manager, Eastern Railway Vs . Salyajit 
Mazumdar ). This being the legal position , I have no hesi 
tation to hold that the concerned workmon are the real 
workpien of the management of Sudandid Cual Washery . 
This being so , there exists relationship of oployer and 
employoe between the management of Sudamdih Coal Wash 
ry and the concerned workmen ,

5 . The I party , on 8th August, 1989, has filed the rejoin 
der In the rejninder he has reaffirmed his case set out in the 
claim statement. It is stated in the rejoinder that the letter 
dated 13th February , 1989 referred to by the I party was 
taken by the workman Devaraj by some top officers by visit 
ing the house of Devaraj. The Union is the I party and the 
offer should have been made to the Union . The IT party 
cannot put an end to the present reference by questionable 
methods. 1 he request of the workman to withdraw the dispute 
has no relevance because the dispule has been raised by the 
Union . 

6 . My Learned Predecessor has framed the following two 
additional issues : 
1 . Whether the refere .zce is not maintainable and is 

reliable to be rejected for the reasons shown in para 
13A of the counter statement ? 
Whether the action of the IT party management 
and the concerned employee have no relevance to the 
dispue and whether the reference is still maintainable 
as contended by the I party in para 5 of the tu 

joinder ? 
y. On behalf of the II party no evidence has been adduced 
( See order sheet dated 27th November, 1990 ) . On behalf of 
the I party W . W . 1 Jayakumar , General Secretary of the Denn 
Bank Fmplovees Union has been examined . Exs. M . 1 to 8 
are marked through W . W . 1 . These documents were produced 
by the Il party . 

8 . The memorandum dated 27th July , 1987 issued to the 
workman Devaraj, unfortunately not marked , is not disputed . 
Its zerox copy is before the Tribunal. As per this order in the 
memorandum the workman has been debarred for a period of 
3 years from holding any allowance carrying post as per the 
settlenent because he refused to accept the offer to perform 
cash - peon duties at Peenya Branch , 

9 . At the outset it is argued by the Learned Counsel for 
the Il party that the reference is not maintainable since the 
period of three years mentioned in his memorandum has elaps 
ed . When the reference lias been made , the Tribunal is bound 
to pass its award , rejecting the reference or accepting the 
reference . If an award is passed in favour of the workman , 
he will be entitled to some benefits like back wages, So there 
is no force in the argument that the reference is not main 
tainable . 


13. The 1 partly General Secretary of Dena Bank Emplo 
yees Union ( W . W . 1) has stated in his evidence that the 
workman Devaraj told him that the letter was obtained from 
him when he was in his house . No doubt he has stated that 
he has got knowledge of the letter . This is not sufficient in 
view of the say of W . W . 1 that Devaraj told him that the 
letter was obtained from him when he was in his house . 

14 . From the discussiou made in the above paras I am of 
opinion , the alleged letter given by workman Devaraj has 
not been proved . So I hold additional issue No . 1 against 
the II party and additional issue No. 2 in favour of the I 
farty . The reference is maintainable and cannot be rejected 
as contended in para 13 of the counter statement. The 
action of the II party management and the workman Devaraj 
have no relevance to the present dispute since the letter has 
not been proved . 

15 . Now the point for consideration is whether the II party 
Bank was justified in debarıing the workman Devaraj, Cleaner 
cum - Sepoy from holding any special allowance carrying post 
for a period of 3 years as per the letter dated 27- 7 - 1987 . 
It is not disputed hat if a cleaner-cum - sepoy refuses to accept 
the post of cash - reon is liable to be debarred for 3 years from 
holding any special allowance carrying post. It is not dis . 
puted that the workman Devaraj refused to accept the most 
of cash - peon when he was offered . 

16 . Ex. M . 6 is the Seniority list of the subordinate staff, 
This shows the seniority of cleaner - cum -sepoy. In the senin 
rity list. the present workman Devavraj stands at No . 28 . 
Ahove him there are four persons belonging to the category 
of the workman Devaraj. They are Mannan (SI. No . 24 ), 
Udayakumar (SI, No . 25 ). K . Venkataiah (Sl. No 26 ), and 
Gopalan (Sl. No . 27 ). Wlien the vacancy of cash peon at 
Peenya arose , it is most significant to note , it was not offered 
to the said four senior persons but it was offered to workman 
Devaraj. The conduct of the II party shows, as rightly con 
tended on behalf of the I party , that it was offered to work 
man Devaraj because Peenya was far off from the residence 
of the workman . The lack of bonafides in the offer made ty 
the II party to the workman Devaraj is writ larg from the 
records. The workman Devaraj cannot be penalised for re 
fusing the offer of cash peon post . 

17 . For the aforesaid reasons the reference has to be 
accepted . 

18 . All other documents and evidence not referred to by 
me are not relevant. In any case they do not alter the conclu 
sions reached by nie above . I have touched the salient 
points . 


AWARD 


The management of Dena Bank was not justified in debtr 
ring the workman Devaraj. Cleaner - cum - Sepoy from holding 
any sfecial allowance carrying post for a period of 3 years 
as per the letter dated 27- 7 - 1987. 


10 . The main argument advanced by the Learned Counsel 
for the II party is that the reference is not maintainable and 
should be rejected because the workman Devaraj has given 
a letter to the II party stating that he would withdraw the 
reference before this Tribunal. In this regard the Learned 
Counsel rclied on the decision of our Hon ble High Court re 
ported in FIR 1982 Page 148 (Mysore Structurals Ltd ., v / s. 
Workmen of Mysore Structurals I td . ) . In this case his Lord 
ship the Hon ble Mr. Justice K . A . Swamy has been pleased 
to lay down that even if the workman is represented by the 
Trade Union (as in this case ), the right of the workman to 
settle his dispute himself with the employer is not taken 
away. The Learned counsel for the I party stated this deci 
sion was not applicable to the facts of the case . But he was 
unable to say how it was not applicable . It is clear from the 
Law laid down by our Hon ble High Court that the workman 
himself can settlement the dispute before the Tribunal, though 
his cause has been championed by the Trade Union . 

11 . But there is great difficulty for this Tribunal to apply 
the law laid down by our Hon ble High Court and give a 
finding in favour of the II party . 

12 . The alleged letter given by the workman Devaraj to 
the II party has been produced before this Tribunal by the 
t party . But it has not been marked and it has not been 
proved according to Law . It was obligatory on the part of 
the II party to examine a proper person to get the letter 
marked and prove it. That has not been done. This alleged 
letter has not been unequivocally admitted by the I party . 
in the rejoinder field by the I party it has been stated that 
the I [ party connot put an end to the dispute before this 
Tribunal by using questionable and objectionable methods . It 
has been stated that the top Officers of the Il party went to 
the house of workman Devaraj and got the alleged leitei , 
This obviously means that the I party has repudiated the lette : 
alleged to have been given voluntarily by the workman Deva . 
Taj to the II praty , 


The workman is entitled to the special allowance it any 
which he was getting in his post cleaner -cum -sepoy . 

Reference accepted as stated herein . 
No costs,

It is alleged in the chargeshiect that Kailash Prasad 
billed LIC. bil in favour of Bulla Sekh and two 
others without mentioning their period of leave and 
that as checking clerk it was the duty of the col 
ceed workman to check whether the leave was 
granted to admit L . T . C . payment. During the 
comestic enquiry the concered workmen did not 
state anything and relied on the statement as made 
out in the reply to the charge - shect. In reply to the 
charge - sheet he stated that it was not required to 
clieck and verify the details of leave from different 
register and lo chack and verify whether lvave was 
granted or not. He was also not required to see if 
a particular workman was on the roll of the coin 
pany or nol . By way of explanation he has stated 
that it he wus legiurid to check every detail of bill 
before final payment was made , it will take hours 
togci hưr lo dispose of an individuai cases. In my 
view , thc explanation provided by the concerned 
Workma in his reply to the charge -shect is sensible , 
flausible and well merited . Every individual clerk 
has his area of operarion and he should be ills 
werable for ar lapse in performance of his duties 
falling within his area of operatio .n. It woull be an 
onerous job for Accounts Assistant as the concerned 
workman was if he was :o check and verify every 
aspect of the bill for which different clc ks were 
assigned different duties . Besides , it cannot escape 
my av: ention that the higher echolous of ihc colliery 
including the Agent cannot avoid their responsi 
bilities by pleading their Innocense in the matter . 
As a responsible officer it is expected of them to 
check and verify som bills at l andum and not to 
sign payment orders for the bills on thc dotted lines . 

11 . Upoi consideration of evidence on record , I 
come to the conclusion that the concerned worknian 
was not guilty of theft, fraud or dishonesty in connec 
tion with compapy s business or property . There is 
no cvidence on record to show that he did any ling 
with intention of causing wrongful gain to himself 
or wrongful loss to this emplover . There is also no 
evidence on record that he had intention to deceive 
his employer . But nevertheless the fact remains that 
he committed somç mistakes and carelessness which 
he should have avoided . 

12 . The sponsoring union has claimed reinstate 
ment of the concerned workman in service . From niy 
discussion above it is evident that the punishent of 
dismissal from service awarded to the concerned 
workman must be set aside. But the question re 
mains if the concerned workman should be rein 
stated in service with full back wages . The effect of 
reinstatement is to restore an employee to his fos 
mer caapcity , status, emoluments and continuity of 
cervice , as if his services had never heen terminated. 
In the absence of cogent reason to the contrary , hc 
is cri itled to compensation which should normally 
be equal to full wage . In the present cace sirom the 
conc- cned workian committed mistake and care 
lessness , he is not entitled to full wages for the 
period he remained dismissed from servicr . In my 
view end of justice will be not if he is allowed 50040 
of his hock war from the date of his dismissal 
from service till he is reinstated
